&=O(1)\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{5}\langle(I-\bar{w}_{j,t}\bar{w}_{j,t}^{\top}+\bar{w}_{j,t}\bar{w}_{j,t}^{\top})w_{3-j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle]\\
&\quad+O(1)\mathbb{E}[E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}\langle w_{j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{2}\langle w_{3-j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{4}]\end{split}

(A.3)

Using Hölder’s inequality and our assumpsion on
\xi_{p}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{5}\langle(I-\bar{w}_{j,t}\bar{w}_{j,t}^{\top})w_{3-j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle]

\displaystyle\lesssim\varrho\|\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{j}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{5}\|\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{3-j}^{(t)}\|_{2}

In the meantime, we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{5}\langle\bar{w}_{j,t}\bar{w}_{j,t}^{\top}w_{3-j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle]=\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}][R_{j}^{(t)}]^{-1/2}[R_{3-j}^{(t)}]^{1/2}=\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})[R_{j}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{3-j}^{(t)}]^{1/2}

for the last term in (
A.3
), we can also use Hölder’s inequality to get

\displaystyle E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{2}\langle w_{3-j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{4}]

\displaystyle\lesssim E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}]^{1/3}\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{3-j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}]^{2/3}\lesssim E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}R_{j}^{(t)}[R_{3-j}^{(t)}]^{2}

Therefore, we can combine above analysis to get

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)},w_{3-j}^{(t)}\rangle=(\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})\pm\varrho)[R_{j}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{3-j}^{(t)}]^{1/2}+O(E_{j,3-j}^{(t)})R_{j}^{(t)}[R_{3-j}^{(t)}]^{2}

When
j^{\prime}=3-j
, we also have

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}\mathcal{E}_{3-j,j}^{(t)},w_{3-j}^{(t)}\rangle

\displaystyle=6\mathbb{E}[(E_{3-j,j}^{(t)})^{2}\langle w_{j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{5}\langle w_{3-j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle+E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}\langle w_{j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{2}\langle w_{3-j}^{(t)},\xi_{p}\rangle^{4}]

\displaystyle=6(E_{3-j,j}^{(t)})^{2}(\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})\pm\varrho)[R_{j}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{3-j}^{(t)}]^{1/2}+E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}R_{j}^{(t)}[R_{3-j}^{(t)}]^{2}

which proves the claim.
∎

Appendix B
Phase I: Learning the Stronger Feature

In this section, we shall discuss the initial phase of learning the stronger feature. Firstly, we establish some properties at the initialization for our induction afterwards.

Initialization properties.

We prove the following properties for our network at initialization. Recall our initialization is
w_{j}^{(0)}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d}/d),\forall j\in[2]
and
E^{(0)}=I_{2}
.

Lemma B.1

(properties at initialization)
.

Recall that without loss of generality we let
|B_{1,1}^{(0)}|=\max_{j\in[2]}|B_{j,1}^{(0)}|
. With probability
1-o(1)
, the following holds:

1.

\|w_{j}^{(0)}\|_{2}^{2}=1\pm\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
for all
j\in[2]
, and
|\langle w_{1}^{(0)},w_{2}^{(0)}\rangle|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
;

2.

\max_{j,\ell}|B_{j,\ell}^{(0)}|\leq O(\sqrt{\log d/d})
and
\min_{j,\ell}|B_{j,\ell}^{(0)}|\geq\Omega(\frac{1}{\log d})\max_{j,\ell}|B_{j,\ell}^{(0)}|
;

3.

|B_{1,1}^{(0)}|\geq|B_{2,1}^{(0)}|(1+\frac{1}{\log d})
;

4.

\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(0)}=(1-O(\frac{1}{d^{3}}))\sigma^{6}\|w_{j}^{(0)}\|_{2}^{6}=\Theta(1)
for all
j\in[2]
;

5.

H_{j,\ell}^{(0)}=C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(0)}(1+\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}))
for all
(j,\ell)\in[2]\times[2]
;

6.

U_{j}^{(0)}=C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(0)}(1+\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{\sqrt{d}}))
for all
j\in[2]
;

7.

(Q_{j}^{(0)})^{-2}=C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(0)}(1+\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{\sqrt{d}}))
for all
j\in[2]
;

8.

K_{j,\ell}^{(0)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{\ell}^{6}/d^{3})
for all
(j,\ell)\in[2]\times[2]
.

Let us first introduce a fact about Gaussian ratio distribution without proof.

Fact B.2

(Gaussian ratio distribution)
.

If
X
and
Y
are two independent standard Gaussian variables, then the probability density of
Z=X/Y
is
p(z)=\frac{1}{\pi(1+z^{2})},z\in(-\infty,\infty)
.

Proof of
Lemma
B.1
.

1.

Norm bound comes from simple
\chi^{2}
concentration inequality and our initialization
w_{j}^{(0)}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,\frac{I_{d}}{d})
. The inner product bound comes from Gaussian concentration.

2.

It is from a direct calculation under our initialization, and some application of Gaussian c.d.f. and a union bound.

3.

It is from a probability distribution of Gaussian ratio distribution from
Fact
B.2
to bound the probability of
|B_{1,1}^{(0)}|/|B_{2,1}^{(0)}|\leq(1+\frac{1}{\log d})
(WLOG we let
|B_{1,1}^{(0)}|=\max_{j\in[2]}|B_{j,1}^{(0)}|
).

4.

It can be directly proven from our assumption on noise
\xi_{p}
in the subspace
V^{\perp}
and (a).

5.

Since at the initialization we have
B_{j,\ell}^{(0)}=\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}),j,\ell\in[2]
and
E_{j,3-j}^{(0)}=0
, it is easy to directly upper bound the errors.

6.

Again from
B_{j,\ell}^{(0)}=\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}),\forall j,\ell\in[2]
at initialization and a direct upper bound.

7.

Proof is similar to (e).

8.

Directly from a naive upper bound using (b).

∎

B.1
Induction in Phase I

We define phase I as all iterations
t\leq T_{1}
, where
T_{1}:=\min\{t:B_{1,1}^{(t)}\geq 0.01\}
, we will prove the existence of
T_{1}
at the end of this section. We state the following induction hypotheses, which will hold throughout the phase I:

Inductions B.3
.

For each
t\leq T_{1}
, all of the followings hold:

1.

\|w_{j}^{(t)}\|_{2}=\|w_{j}^{(0)}\|_{2}\pm\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
for each
j\in[2]
;

2.

|B_{1,2}^{(t)}|,|B_{2,1}^{(t)}|,|B_{2,2}^{(t)}|=\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
;

3.

|B_{1,1}^{(t)}|\geq\Omega(\frac{1}{\log d})\max(|B_{1,2}^{(t)}|,|B_{2,2}^{(t)}|,|B_{2,1}^{(t)}|)
;

4.

|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})\frac{\eta_{E}}{\eta}|B_{1,1}^{(t)}|
and
|E_{2,1}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d})
;

5.

R_{1}^{(t)},R_{2}^{(t)}=\Theta(1)
,
|R_{1,2}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})

Remark B.4
.

Since we have chosen
\eta_{E}\leq\eta
and
\varrho\leq\frac{1}{d^{\Omega(1)}}
,
Induction
B.3
d
implies
|E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}|=o(1)
throughout
t\leq T_{1}
.

We shall prove the above induction holds in later sections, but first we need some useful claims assuming our induction holds in this phase.

B.2
Computing Variables at Phase I

Firstly we establish a claim controlling the noise terms
\mathcal{E}_{j},\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}
during this phase.

Claim B.5
.

At each iteration
t\leq T_{1}
, if
Induction
B.3
holds, then

1.

\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(t)}=\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(t)}\pm O(\sum_{\ell\in[2]}|B_{j,\ell}^{(t)}|+\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}))

2.

\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}=\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(0)}\pm O(\sum_{\ell\in[2]}|B_{j,\ell}^{(t)}|+\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}))

3.

\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)}=\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}\pm\widetilde{O}(E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})+(E_{j,3-j}^{(t)})^{2})
;

Proof.

For (a), we can simply write down

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}=\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}]=\sigma^{6}\|\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{j}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{6}

Note that by
Induction
B.3
a
we always have
\|w_{j}^{(t)}\|_{2}=\|w_{j}^{(0)}\|_{2}\pm\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
, and by
Lemma
B.1
a
we also have
\|w_{j}^{(0)}\|_{2}=(1\pm\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}))\|w_{j}^{(0)}\|_{2}
, which implies

\displaystyle\|\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{j}^{(t)}\|_{2}-\|\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{3-j}^{(t)}\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\|w_{j}^{(t)}\|_{2}-\|w_{3-j}^{(t)}\|_{2}\pm O(\sum_{j,\ell\in[2]^{2}}B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\|w_{j}^{(0)}\|_{2}-\|w_{3-j}^{(0)}\|_{2}\pm O(\sum_{j,\ell\in[2]^{2}}B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})\pm\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})

\displaystyle=\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})\pm O(\sum_{j,\ell\in[2]^{2}}B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})\pm\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})

By the elementary equality
x^{n}-y^{n}=(x-y)\sum_{0\leq i\leq n-1}x^{i}y^{n-1-i}
, we can obtain (a). The proof of (b) is almost the same as (a), and the proof of (c) is just direct calculation.
∎

Equipped with
Claim
B.5
, we can establish the following lemma, which will be frequently applied to bound the gradient in our induction argument.

Lemma B.6

(variables control in phase I)
.

Suppose Induction
B.3
holds at some iteration
t\leq T_{1}
, then we have:

1.

if
\forall\ell\in[2],\alpha_{\ell}|B_{j,\ell}^{(t)}|\leq O(1)
, then
\Phi_{j}^{(t)}=(C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)})^{-2}(1\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})
;

2.

if
\exists\ell\in[2],|B_{j,\ell}^{(t)}|\geq\Omega(\frac{1}{\alpha_{\ell}})
, then
\Phi_{j}^{(t)}=O((C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}+\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{1}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{6})^{-2})
;

3.

if
\alpha_{\ell}|B_{j,\ell}^{(t)}|\leq O(1)
,
H_{j,\ell}^{(t)}=C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}(1+\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})=\Theta(C_{2})
, otherwise
H_{j,\ell}^{(t)}\in[\Omega(C_{2}),\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{\ell}^{6})]

4.

|K_{j,\ell}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{\ell}^{6}/d^{3/2})

Proof.

1.

From our assumptions that
|B_{1,2}^{(t)}|,|B_{2,1}^{(t)}|,|B_{2,2}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
and
\alpha_{1}B_{1,1}^{(t)}\leq O(1)
, and also the fact that
\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}=\Omega(\sigma^{6})=\Omega(1)
,
C_{2}=\Theta(\mathsf{polylog}(d))\gg C_{1}
, we can calculate

\displaystyle U_{j}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{1}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}((B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3})^{2}+C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=O(C_{1})+C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}+\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})

\displaystyle=C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}(1\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})

Meanwhile, we can also compute similarly

\displaystyle Q_{j}^{(t)}=\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{1}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{6}+C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}=C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}(1\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})

Therefore
\Phi_{j}^{(t)}=Q_{j}^{(t)}/(U_{j}^{(t)})^{3/2}=(C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}(1\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)}))^{-2}
as desired.

2.

The proof is similar to that of (a).

3.

when
\alpha_{1}B_{1,1}^{(t)}\leq O(1)
, the proof is similar to (a). When
\alpha_{1}B_{1,1}^{(t)}\geq O(1)
, we have from
Induction
B.3
a
and
H_{j,\ell}^{(t)}
’s expression that

\displaystyle H_{j,\ell}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{1}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}((B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3})^{2}+C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{\ell}^{6})

And since
T_{1}:=\min\{t:B_{1,1}^{(t)}\geq 0.01\}
, so for
t\leq T_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle H_{j,\ell}^{(t)}\geq C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{①}}}{{\geq}}C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}-|E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}|\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{②}}}{{\geq}}\Omega(C_{2})

where ① is from
Claim
B.5
b
and ② is from
Induction
B.3
d
.

4.

Since we have assumed
|B_{1,2}^{(t)}|,|B_{2,1}^{(t)}|,|B_{2,2}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
, it is direct to bound
|K_{j,\ell}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{\ell}^{6}/d^{1.5})
.

∎

Claim B.7

(about
\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}
and
\nabla_{w_{j}}\mathcal{E}_{j^{\prime},3-j^{\prime}}^{(t)}
)
.

If
Induction
B.3
holds at iteration
t\leq T_{1}
, then

1.

\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}=O(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})\frac{(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{6}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}}\frac{\Phi_{j}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}
;

2.

\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)},w_{j}^{(t)}\rangle=O(1)\pm O(E_{j,3-j}^{(t)})(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)
;

3.

\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}\mathcal{E}_{3-j,j}^{(t)},w_{j}^{(t)}\rangle=O((E_{3-j,j}^{(t)})^{2})\pm O(E_{3-j,j}^{(t)})(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)

4.

|\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)},w_{3-j}^{(t)}\rangle|=O(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)+O(E_{j,3-j}^{(t)})
;

5.

|\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}\mathcal{E}_{3-j,j}^{(t)},w_{3-j}^{(t)}\rangle|=O(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)(E_{3-j,j}^{(t)})^{2}+O(E_{3-j,j}^{(t)})

Proof.

Notice that
\|\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{j}^{(t)}\|_{2}=\Theta(1),\forall j\in[2]
for
t\leq T_{1}
, which is because of
\|w_{j}^{(t)}\|_{2}=\sqrt{2}\pm o(1)
from
Induction
B.3
a
and
\max_{j,\ell}|B_{j,\ell}^{(t)}|<0.02
4
4

4

due to our choice of
\eta=\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}
is small, we can make sure when
T_{1}=\min\{t:B_{1,1}^{(t)}\geq 0.01\}
,
B_{1,1}^{(T_{1})}<0.02
.
. Now we can apply
Claim
A.1
to obtain the bounds.
∎

B.3
Gradient Lemmas for Phase I

We first present an interesting lemma regarding the effects of Batch-Normalization on the gradients of weights. The following lemma allow us maintain the norm of weights to above a constant throughout phase I.

Lemma B.8

(effects of BN on gradients)
.

For any
W=(w_{1},w_{2})
and
E
, it holds

(a)

\sum_{j\in[2]}\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W,E),w_{j}\rangle=0
;

Further, if
Induction
B.3
holds for each
t\leq T_{1}
, we have

(b)

|\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),w_{j}^{(t)}\rangle|\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})|\Lambda_{1,1}|\sum_{j\in[2]}|E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}|
for each
j\in[2]
.

Proof.

Proof of (a):
We first calculate the gradient term as follows:

\displaystyle\nabla_{W}L(W,E)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{W}\sum_{j\in[2]}\frac{\mathbb{E}[F_{j}(X^{(1)})\cdot\mathsf{StopGrad}[G_{j}(X^{(2)})]]}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[F_{j}^{2}(X^{(1)})]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{StopGrad}[G_{j}^{2}(X^{(2)})]]}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j\in[2]}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(\nabla_{W}F_{j}(X^{(1)}))\cdot[G(X^{(2)})]_{j}]\cdot\mathbb{E}[F_{j}^{2}(X^{(1)})]}{(\mathbb{E}[F_{j}^{2}(X^{(1)})])^{3/2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[G_{j}^{2}(X^{(2)})]}}

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{j\in[2]}\frac{\mathbb{E}[(\nabla_{W}F_{j}(X^{(1)}))\cdot F_{j}(X^{(1)})]\cdot\mathbb{E}[[F_{j}(X^{(1)})\cdot[G(X^{(2)})]_{j}]}{(\mathbb{E}[F_{j}^{2}(X^{(1)})])^{3/2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[G_{j}^{2}(X^{(2)})]}}

Since by our definition
\langle\nabla_{W}F_{j}(X^{(1)}),W\rangle=\sum_{i\in[2]}\langle\nabla_{w_{i}}[F_{j}(X^{(1)}),w_{i}\rangle=3[F_{j}(X^{(1)})
, we immediately have
\sum_{j\in[2]}\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W,E),w_{j}\rangle=0
.

Proof of (b):
Firstly we define a new notion

\displaystyle\nabla_{i,j}=\nabla_{w_{i}}\frac{\mathbb{E}[F_{j}(X^{(1)})\cdot\mathsf{StopGrad}[G_{j}(X^{(2)})]]}{\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[F_{j}^{2}(X^{(1)})]}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\mathsf{StopGrad}[G_{j}^{2}(X^{(2)})]]}}

Then it is straghtforward to verify that
\sum_{i\in[2]}\langle\nabla_{i,j},w_{i}\rangle=0
for any
j\in[2]
, which implies that
|\langle\nabla_{j^{\prime},j},w_{j^{\prime}}\rangle|=|\langle\nabla_{3-j^{\prime},j},w_{3-j^{\prime}}\rangle|
. So in order to obtain an upper bound for
|\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W,E),w_{j}\rangle|=|\sum_{j^{\prime}\in[2]}\langle\nabla_{j,j^{\prime}},w_{j}\rangle|
, we only need to upper bound
|\langle\nabla_{j,j^{\prime}},w_{3-j^{\prime}}\rangle|
, each of which can be calculated as (ignoring all time superscript
(t)
)

\displaystyle|\langle\nabla_{3-j,j},w_{3-j}\rangle|

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{p\in[P]\cap\mathcal{P}}E_{j,3-j}\sigma(\langle w_{3-j},X_{p}\rangle)\cdot[G(X^{(2)})]_{j}\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}[F_{j}^{2}(X^{(1)})]}{(\mathbb{E}[F_{j}^{2}(X^{(1)})])^{3/2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[G_{j}^{2}(X^{(2)})]}}

\displaystyle\quad-\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{p\in[P]\cap\mathcal{P}}E_{j,3-j}\sigma(\langle w_{3-j},X_{p}\rangle)\cdot F_{j}(X^{(1)})\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}[[F_{j}(X^{(1)})\cdot[G(X^{(2)})]_{j}]}{(\mathbb{E}[F_{j}^{2}(X^{(1)})])^{3/2}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[G_{j}^{2}(X^{(2)})]}}

Now we compute

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{p\in[P]\cap\mathcal{P}}E_{j,3-j}\sigma(\langle w_{3-j},X_{p}\rangle)[G(X^{(2)})]_{j}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{p\in[P]\cap\mathcal{P}}E_{j,3-j}\sigma(\langle w_{3-j},X_{p}\rangle)\sum_{p\in[P]\setminus\mathcal{P}}\sigma(\langle w_{j},X_{p}\rangle)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in[2]}E_{j,3-j}C_{0}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}B_{j,\ell}^{3}

and

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{p\in[P]\cap\mathcal{P}}E_{j,3-j}\sigma(\langle w_{3-j},X_{p}\rangle)\cdot F_{j}(X^{(1)})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{p\in[P]\cap\mathcal{P}}E_{j,3-j}\sigma(\langle w_{3-j},X_{p}\rangle)\cdot\sum_{p\in[P]\cap\mathcal{P}}\left(\sigma(\langle w_{j},X_{p}\rangle)+E_{j,3-j}\sigma(\langle w_{3-j},X_{p}\rangle)\right)\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in[2]}E_{j,3-j}C_{1}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{3}+E_{j,3-j}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3})+C_{2}E_{j,3-j}\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}+E_{j,3-j}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}]

So we can further obtain the nominator in the expression of
|\langle\nabla_{3-j,j},w_{3-j}\rangle|
as

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{p\in[P]\cap\mathcal{P}}E_{j,3-j}\sigma(\langle w_{3-j},X_{p}\rangle)\cdot[G(X^{(2)})]_{j}\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}[F_{j}^{2}(X^{(1)})]

\displaystyle\quad-\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{p\in[P]\cap\mathcal{P}}E_{j,3-j}\sigma(\langle w_{3-j},X_{p}\rangle)\cdot F_{j}(X^{(1)})\right]\cdot\mathbb{E}[[F_{j}(X^{(1)})\cdot[G(X^{(2)})]_{j}]

\displaystyle=\left(\sum_{\ell\in[2]}E_{j,3-j}C_{0}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}B_{j,\ell}^{3}\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{1}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}(B_{j,\ell}^{3}+E_{j,3-j}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3})^{2}+C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\sum_{\ell\in[2]}E_{j,3-j}C_{1}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{3}+E_{j,3-j}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3})\right)\cdot\left(\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{0}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{j,\ell}^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{3}+E_{j,3-j}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3})\right)

\displaystyle\quad-C_{2}E_{j,3-j}\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}+E_{j,3-j}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}]\cdot\left(\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{0}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{j,\ell}^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{3}+E_{j,3-j}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3})\right)

\displaystyle=E_{j,3-j}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{0}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{3}H_{j,3-\ell}-B_{j,3-\ell}^{3}K_{j,3-\ell})

\displaystyle\quad-C_{2}E_{j,3-j}\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}+E_{j,3-j}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}]\cdot\left(\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{0}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{j,\ell}^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{3}+E_{j,3-j}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3})\right)

Now can sum over
j^{\prime}\in[2]
to get

\displaystyle\quad|\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W,E),w_{j}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[2]}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{0}E_{j,3-j}\left|\Phi_{j}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}B_{j,\ell}^{3}H_{j,3-\ell}\right|+\sum_{j\in[2]}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\left|C_{0}E_{j,3-j}\Phi_{j}\alpha_{\ell}^{3}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}B_{j,3-\ell}^{3}K_{j,3-\ell}\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{j\in[2]}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\left|C_{2}E_{j,3-j}\Phi_{j}\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}+E_{j,3-j}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}]C_{0}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{j,\ell}^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{3}+E_{j,3-j}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3})\right|

Next we are going to bound each term, for the first term of LHS we have

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[2]}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\left|C_{0}E_{j,3-j}\Phi_{j}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}B_{j,\ell}^{3}H_{j,3-\ell}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[2]}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}|E_{j,3-j}||\Lambda_{j,\ell}|\left|\frac{B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}}{B_{j,\ell}^{2}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\Lambda_{1,1}|\sum_{j\in[2]}|E_{j,3-j}||\left|\frac{B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}B_{j,\ell}^{3}\Phi_{j}}{B_{1,1}^{5}\Phi_{1}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{d^{o(1)}}{\sqrt{d}})|\Lambda_{1,1}|\sum_{j\in[2]}|E_{j,3-j}|

where the last inequality is because

•

By
Lemma
B.6
a,b
, we have
\Phi_{j}^{(t)}/\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\leq O(\alpha_{1}^{O}(1))\leq d^{o(1)}
during
t\leq T_{1}
.

•

(B_{3-j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{5}
from
Induction
B.3
b,c
.

Similarly, we can also compute

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[2]}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\left|C_{0}E_{j,3-j}\Phi_{j}\alpha_{\ell}^{3}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}B_{j,3-\ell}^{3}K_{j,3-\ell}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j\in[2]}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}E_{j,3-j}|\Lambda_{1,1}|\left|\frac{B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}B_{j,3-\ell}^{3}K_{j,3-\ell}}{B_{1,1}^{5}H_{j,3-\ell}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{d^{o(1)}}{d^{2}})|\Lambda_{1,1}|\sum_{j\in[2]}|E_{j,3-j}|

and

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[2]}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\left|C_{2}E_{j,3-j}\Phi_{j}\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}+E_{j,3-j}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}]C_{0}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{j,\ell}^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{3}+E_{j,3-j}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3})\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{①}}}{{\leq}}\

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[2]}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}|E_{j,3-j}\Lambda_{j,\ell}|\left|\frac{B_{j,\ell}^{3}+E_{j,3-j}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}}{B_{j,\ell}^{2}}\right|\left|\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}+E_{j,3-j}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}]\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{②}}}{{\leq}}\

\displaystyle\sum_{j\in[2]}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}|E_{j,3-j}\Lambda_{j,\ell}|\left|\frac{B_{j,\ell}^{3}+E_{j,3-j}B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}}{B_{j,\ell}^{2}}\right|(O(R_{1,2}+\varrho)+O(E_{j,3-j}))

\displaystyle\leq\

\displaystyle\widetilde{O}(R_{1,2}+\varrho)|\Lambda_{1,1}|\sum_{j\in[2]}|E_{j,3-j}|

where ① is due to
Lemma
B.6
c
, ② is from the same calculation in
Claim
B.7
for
\mathbb{E}[\langle w_{j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}]
and
Induction
B.3
a
. Now combining the above and
Induction
B.3
e
together we have

\displaystyle|\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W,E),w_{j}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})|\Lambda_{1,1}|\sum_{j\in[2]}|E_{j,3-j}|

which gives the desired bound.
∎

Next we give a lemma characterizing the gradient of feature
v_{1}
in this phase.

Lemma B.9

(learning feature
v_{1}
in phase I)
.

For each
t\leq T_{1}
, if Induction
B.3
holds at iteration
t
, then using notations of (
A.2
), we have:

1.

\langle-\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),v_{1}\rangle=(1\pm\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d}))\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}

2.

\langle-\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),v_{1}\rangle=(1\pm O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}))\Lambda_{2,1}^{(t)}+\Gamma_{2,1}^{(t)}\leq(1\pm O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}))\Lambda_{2,1}^{(t)}\pm\frac{(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}}E_{1,2}^{(t)}\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}

Proof.

From (
A.2
), we write down the gradient formula for
B_{j,1}^{(t)}
as follows:

\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{w_{j}}L_{\mathcal{D}}(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),v_{1}\rangle

\displaystyle=\Lambda_{j,1}^{(t)}+\Gamma_{j,1}^{(t)}-\Upsilon_{j,1}^{(t)}

where (ignoring the superscript
(t)
for the RHS)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{j,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\Phi_{j}\alpha_{1}^{6}B_{j,1}^{5}H_{j,2}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\Phi_{3-j}E_{3-j,j}\alpha_{1}^{6}B_{3-j,1}^{3}B_{j,1}^{2}H_{3-j,2}

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{j,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\left(\Phi_{j}B_{j,2}^{3}B_{j,1}^{2}K_{j,1}+\Phi_{3-j}E_{3-j,j}B_{3-j,2}^{3}B_{j,1}^{2}K_{3-j,1}\right)

We first prove (a), and we deal with each term individually:

Comparing
\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}
and
\Gamma_{1,1}^{(t)}
:
When
t\leq T_{1,1}
, we have from
Lemma
B.6
a
that

\Phi_{1}^{(t)}H_{1,2}^{(t)}=\frac{1}{C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(t)}}(1\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})=\frac{1}{C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(t)}}(1\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})=\Phi_{2}^{(t)}H_{2,2}^{(t)}(1\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})

Further, by
Induction
B.3
b,c,d
and our definition of stage 1, we know
E_{1,2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d})
. Now from
Induction
B.3
b
that
B_{2,1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
, together we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1,1}^{(t)}=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}E_{2,1}^{(t)}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}H_{2,2}^{(t)}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d})C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}H_{1,2}^{(t)}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{5}=\widetilde{O}(\frac{\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}}{d})

When
t\in[T_{1,1},T_{1}]
, by
Lemma
B.6
b

we have

\displaystyle\Phi_{1}^{(t)}H_{1,2}^{(t)}\geq\Omega(\frac{C_{2}}{(C_{1}\alpha_{1}^{6}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}+O(C_{2}))^{2}})\geq\omega(\frac{1}{d^{0.1}}),\quad\text{and}\quad E_{2,1}^{(t)}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}H_{2,2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d})

Now from our definition of stage 2, it holds that
B_{1,1}^{(t)}\geq\Omega(\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}})
while
B_{2,1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
by
Induction
B.3
b
, which gives

\displaystyle\Gamma_{1,1}^{(t)}=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}E_{2,1}^{(t)}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}H_{2,2}^{(t)}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d})C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}H_{1,2}^{(t)}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{5}=\widetilde{O}(\frac{\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}}{d})

Comparing
\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}
and
\Upsilon_{1,1}^{(t)}
:
Now consider
\Upsilon_{1,1}^{(t)}
, by
Lemma
B.6
, we can follow the same analysis as above to get

\displaystyle\Phi_{j}^{(t)}K_{j,\ell}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}}{d^{3/2}})\Phi_{1}^{(t)}H_{1,2}^{(t)}

for any
(j,\ell)\in[2]\times[2]

Combined with
E_{2,1}^{(t)}\leq o(1)
, we can derive

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{1,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\left(\Phi_{1}^{(t)}K_{1,1}^{(t)}(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}+E_{1,2}^{(t)}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}K_{2,1}^{(t)}(B_{2,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}\alpha_{2}^{6}}{d^{3/2}})C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}H_{1,2}^{(t)}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{5}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{O}(\frac{\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}}{d^{3/2-o(1)}})

(since
C_{1}=\widetilde{O}(1)
and
\alpha_{1},\alpha_{2}=d^{o(1)}
)

Comparing
\Lambda_{2,1}^{(t)}
and
\Upsilon_{2,1}^{(t)}
:
Till now (a) is proved, we can deal with (b) by only comparing
\Lambda_{2,1}^{(t)}
with
\Upsilon_{2,1}^{(t)}
. Similar to the above arguments, we have by
Induction
B.3
b
we know
K_{j,1}^{(t)}=\widetilde{O}(\frac{C_{1}\alpha_{1}^{6}}{d^{3/2}}),\forall j\in[2]
, and thus

\displaystyle\Phi_{j}^{(t)}K_{j,\ell}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{d^{3/2}})\Phi_{2}^{(t)}H_{2,2}^{(t)}

for any
(j,\ell)\in[2]\times[2]

By
Induction
B.3
e
we know
E_{1,2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
. Also, note that from
Induction
B.3
b
we have
\widetilde{O}((B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}/d)\leq\widetilde{O}((B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{5})
, and thus

\displaystyle E_{1,2}^{(t)}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}K_{1,1}^{(t)}(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{d^{5/2}})\Phi_{2}^{(t)}H_{2,2}^{(t)}\widetilde{O}(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}\leq O(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})\Phi_{2}^{(t)}H_{2,2}^{(t)}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{5}

So together we have

\displaystyle|\Upsilon_{2,1}^{(t)}|

\displaystyle=|C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\left(\Phi_{2}^{(t)}K_{2,1}^{(t)}(B_{2,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}+E_{2,1}^{(t)}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}K_{1,1}^{(t)}(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}\right)|

\displaystyle\leq O(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}H_{2,2}^{(t)}|(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{5}|

\displaystyle=O(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})|\Lambda_{2,1}^{(t)}|

Comparing
\Gamma_{2,1}^{(t)}
with
\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}
:
It suffices to notice that

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{2,1}^{(t)}|\leq|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}H_{1,2}^{(t)}|B_{1,1}^{(t)}|^{3}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}=\frac{(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}}|E_{1,2}^{(t)}||\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}|

Combining the bounds for
\Lambda_{2,1}^{(t)}
and
\Gamma_{2,1}^{(t)}
, we obtain the proof of (b).
∎

Then we can also calculate the gradients of feature
v_{2}
in this phase.

Lemma B.10

(learning feature
v_{2}
in phase I)
.

For each
t\leq T_{1}
, if Induction
B.3
holds at iteration
t
, then using notations of (
A.2
), we have for each
j\in[2]
:

\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),v_{2}\rangle=\left(1\pm\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{6})(E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}+(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3})\right)\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}

(B.1)

Proof.

Again as in the proof of
Lemma
B.9
, we expand the notations: (ignoring the superscript
(t)
for the RHS)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}H_{j,1}B_{j,2}^{5}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}E_{3-j,j}B_{3-j,2}^{3}B_{j,2}^{2}H_{3-j,1}

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{j,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\left(\Phi_{j}B_{j,1}^{3}B_{j,2}^{2}K_{j,2}+\Phi_{3-j}E_{3-j,j}B_{3-j,1}^{3}B_{j,2}^{2}K_{3-j,2}\right)

We first compare
\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}
and
\Gamma_{j,2}^{(t)}
as follows:
Lemma
B.6
we have

•

B_{3-j,2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})
by
Induction
B.3
b
;

•

From
Lemma
B.6
a,b
we can have
\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{O(1)})\Phi_{j}^{(t)},\forall j\in[2]
.

Together they imply:

\displaystyle C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}H_{3-j,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)})C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}H_{j,2}^{(t)}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{5}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}E_{j,3-j}^{(t)})\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}

(B.2)

Now we turn to compare
\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}
with
\Upsilon_{j,2}^{(t)}
. We split
\Upsilon_{j,2}^{(t)}
into two terms
\Upsilon_{j,2,1}^{(t)},\Upsilon_{j,2,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{j,2,1}^{(t)}=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}K_{j,2}^{(t)},\quad\Upsilon_{j,2,2}^{(t)}=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}K_{3-j,2}^{(t)}

For
\Upsilon_{j,2,1}^{(t)}
, we can calculate

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{j,2,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}K_{j,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{C_{1}\alpha_{2}^{6}}{d^{3/2}})(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3}\cdot C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}H_{j,1}^{(t)}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{6}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3})C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}H_{j,1}^{(t)}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{5}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{6}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3})\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}

(B.3)

And for
\Upsilon_{j,2,1}^{(t)}
, we use
Induction
B.3
b
and
Lemma
B.6
d
again to get

\displaystyle(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{3-j,2}^{(t)})^{2}K_{3-j,2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(C_{1}\alpha_{2}^{6}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{5})

and thus combined with
\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{6})\Phi_{j}^{(t)},\forall j\in[2]
from
Lemma
B.6
a,b
, we can derive

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{j,2,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}K_{3-j,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{6}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)})C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}H_{j,1}^{(t)}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{5}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{6}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)})\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}

(B.4)

Now combine the results of (
B.2
), (
B.3
) and (
B.4
) finishes the proof of (
B.1
).
∎

Lemma B.11

(learning prediction head
E_{1,2},E_{2,1}
in phase I)
.

If
Induction
B.3
holds at iteration
t\leq T_{1}
, then we have

1.

-\nabla_{E_{1,2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})=O(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})\left(-O(E_{1,2}^{(t)})+\widetilde{O}(\frac{(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}})+O(R_{1,2}^{(t)})\right)
;

2.

-\nabla_{E_{2,1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})=\widetilde{O}(\frac{(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}})\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}+\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{2}\Lambda_{2,\ell}^{(t)}B_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\left(-O(E_{2,1}^{(t)})+O(R_{1,2}^{(t)})\right)

Proof.

We first write down the gradient for
E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}
: (ignoring the time superscript
(t)
)

\displaystyle-\nabla_{E_{j,3-j}}L(W,E)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{0}\Phi_{j}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{j,\ell}^{3}(B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}H_{j,3-\ell}-B_{3-j,3-\ell}^{3}K_{j,3-\ell})-\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{j,\ell}\nabla_{E_{j,3-j}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}

where
\nabla_{E_{j,3-j}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}=\mathbb{E}\left[2\langle w_{j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}+2E_{j,3-j}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}\right]
. Thus we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{E_{j,3-j}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)}=O(1)E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}+O(R_{1,2}^{(t)})

and by
Claim
B.5
and
Lemma
B.6
a,b

\displaystyle\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}=O(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})\frac{(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{6}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}}\frac{\Phi_{j}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}\leq O(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})

Now let us look at
\nabla_{E_{1,2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})
, first we consider the term

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{0}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}(B_{1,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}((B_{2,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}H_{1,3-\ell}^{(t)}-(B_{2,3-\ell}^{(t)})^{3}K_{1,3-\ell}^{(t)})

Using
Lemma
B.6
and
Induction
B.3
b,c
, we know

•

H_{1,1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(H_{1,2}^{(t)})
at
t\leq T_{1,1}
and
H_{1,1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{6}H_{1,2}^{(t)})
for
t\in[T_{1,1},T_{1}]
;

•

B_{2,1}^{(t)},B_{1,2}^{(t)},B_{2,2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})\leq\widetilde{O}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})
;

•

K_{1,3-\ell}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{6}/d^{3/2})
.

It can be computed that

\displaystyle C_{0}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\alpha_{2}^{6}(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,2}^{(t)})^{3}H_{1,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(1)\left(\frac{B_{2,1}^{(t)}}{B_{1,1}^{(t)}}\right)^{3}C_{0}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\alpha_{1}^{3}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}H_{1,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\left|C_{0}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}(B_{1,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}K_{1,3-\ell}^{(t)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{d^{3/2}})\frac{(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}}C_{0}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\alpha_{1}^{6}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}H_{1,2}^{(t)}

Now we turn to
\nabla_{E_{2,1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})
, similarly we have

\displaystyle C_{0}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}\alpha_{1}^{6}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}H_{2,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(1)\left(\frac{B_{2,1}^{(t)}}{B_{1,1}^{(t)}}\right)^{3}C_{0}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\alpha_{1}^{6}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}H_{1,2}^{(t)}

and since
H_{2,1}^{(t)}\leq O(C_{2})=O(H_{1,2}^{(t)})
by
Lemma
B.6
c
, we can go through the same arguments again to obtain

\displaystyle\left|C_{0}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}\alpha_{2}^{6}(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,2}^{(t)})^{3}H_{2,1}^{(t)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(1)\left(\frac{B_{1,2}^{(t)}}{B_{1,1}^{(t)}}\right)^{3}C_{0}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\alpha_{1}^{6}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}H_{1,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\left|C_{0}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}\alpha_{2}^{6}(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{3}K_{2,1}^{(t)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{d^{3/2}})\left(\frac{B_{1,2}^{(t)}}{B_{1,1}^{(t)}}\right)^{3}C_{0}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\alpha_{1}^{6}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}H_{1,2}^{(t)}

Now the proof is complete.
∎

Also, we will need the following lemma controlling gradient bounds for the noise term.

Lemma B.12

(update of
R_{1,2}^{(t)}
in phase I)
.

Suppose
Induction
B.3
holds at iteration
t\leq T_{1}
, then we have

1.

|\langle-\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}+\varrho)\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)}

2.

|\langle-\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}+\varrho)\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)}

Proof.

Proof of (a):
Firstly, by
Claim
B.7
a
, we can directly write

\displaystyle\quad\langle\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle=-\sum_{j,\ell}\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}\langle\nabla_{w_{1}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)},w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)}\sum_{(j,\ell)\in[2]^{2}}\frac{(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{6}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}}\frac{\Phi_{j}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}\langle\nabla_{w_{1}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)},w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle

(B.5)

Now we discuss each summand respectively: for
(j,\ell)=(1,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{6}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}}=1+E_{1,2}^{(t)}\frac{(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}}=1+o(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}})

(B.6)

where the last one is due to
Induction
B.3
d
.
And for
\ell=2
, we can see from
Induction
B.3
b and d
, that
\max_{(j,\ell)\neq(1,1)}|B_{j,\ell}^{(t)}|=\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
and
E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}\leq o(1)
to give

\displaystyle\frac{(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{6}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}}\frac{\Phi_{j}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{3}})\frac{1}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}}\frac{\Phi_{j}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}

On one hand, when
t\leq T_{1,1}
, we have
\alpha_{\ell}B_{j,\ell}^{(t)}\leq O(1)
for all
(j,\ell)\in[2]^{2}
, so
Lemma
B.6
a
applies for both
\Phi_{j}^{(t)}
and results in
\Phi_{2}^{(t)}/\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\leq O(1)
. We can also apply
Induction
B.3
c
to have
B_{j,2}^{(t)}/B_{1,1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(1)
. On the other hand, when
t\in[T_{1,1},T_{1}]
, we have by
Induction
B.3
b
and
Lemma
B.6
a,b
that
\Phi_{2}^{(t)}/\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{O(1)})=d^{o(1)}
, but now
B_{1,1}^{(t)}=d^{-o(1)}\gg\widetilde{O}(d^{-1/2})
, therefore

\displaystyle\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{3}})\frac{1}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}}\frac{\Phi_{2}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})\frac{1}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}}

So together, they imply

\displaystyle\frac{(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{6}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}}\frac{\Phi_{j}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}})

(B.7)

and similarly, we have

\displaystyle\frac{(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{6}+E_{2,1}^{(t)}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}}\frac{\Phi_{2}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}})

(B.8)

Next we turn to
\langle\nabla_{w_{1}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)},w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle
. When
j=1
, we can apply
Claim
B.7
d
to get

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{w_{1}}\mathcal{E}_{1,2}^{(t)},w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle=O(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)+O(E_{1,2}^{(t)})=O(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})+O(E_{1,2}^{(t)})\leq O(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})

(B.9)

and when
j=2
, we can apply
Claim
B.7
e
to get

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{w_{1}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{(t)},w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle=-(E_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}O(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)+O(E_{2,1})=\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{2}})(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})+O(\frac{1}{d})

(B.10)

Combining (
B.5
), (
B.6
), (
B.7
), (
B.8
), (
B.9
), and (
B.10
) completes the proof of (a).

Proof of (b):
The
\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}
part is the same as in the proof of (a), so we only deal with
\langle\nabla_{w_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{1,2}^{(t)},w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle
and
\langle\nabla_{w_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{(t)},w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle
here. For
\langle\nabla_{w_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{(t)},w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle
, we apply
Claim
B.7
d
to get

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{w_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{(t)},w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle=O(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)+O(1)E_{1,2}^{(t)}

(B.11)

and for
\langle\nabla_{w_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{1,2}^{(t)},w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle
, we have

\displaystyle\langle\nabla_{w_{2}}\mathcal{E}_{1,2}^{(t)},w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle=O(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)(E_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}+O(1)E_{2,1}^{(t)}

(B.12)

Inserting (
B.6
), (
B.7
), (
B.8
) and (
B.11
), (
B.12
) into the expression of
\langle-\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle
finishes the proof of (b).
∎

B.4
At the End of Phase I

Lemma B.13

(Phase I)
.

Suppose
\eta\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}
is sufficiently small, then
Induction
B.3
holds for at least all
t\leq T_{1}=O(\frac{d^{2}}{\eta})
, and at iteration
t=T_{1}
, we have

1.

B_{1,1}^{(T_{1})}=\Omega(1)
;

2.

\|w_{j}^{(T_{1})}\|_{2}=1\pm\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
;

3.

B_{2,1}^{(T_{1})}=\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
and
B_{j,2}^{(T_{1})}=B_{j,2}^{(0)}(1\pm o(1))
for
j\in[2]
;

4.

E_{2,1}^{(T_{1})}=\widetilde{O}(\frac{\eta_{E}/\eta}{d})
and
E_{1,2}^{(T_{1})}\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
;

5.

R_{1,1}^{(T_{1})},R_{2}^{(T_{1})}=\Theta(1)
and
R_{1,2}^{(T_{1})}=\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
.

Proof.

We begin by first prove the existence of
T_{1}:=\min\{t:B_{1,1}^{(t)}\geq 0.01\}=O(\frac{d^{2}}{\eta})
if
Induction
B.3
holds whenever
B_{1,1}^{(t)}\leq 0.01
, then we will turn back to prove
Induction
B.3
holds throughout
t\leq T_{1}
. We split the analysis into two stages:

Proof of
T_{1}\leq O(\frac{d^{2}}{\eta})
:
By
Lemma
B.9
a
we can write down the update of
B_{1,1}^{(t)}
as

\displaystyle B_{1,1}^{(t+1)}=B_{1,1}^{(t)}+\eta(1\pm\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d}))\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}=B_{1,1}^{(t)}+\eta(1\pm\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d}))\Phi_{1}^{(t)}C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}H_{1,2}^{(t)}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{5}

(B.13)

When
\alpha_{1}B_{1,1}^{(t)}\leq O(1)
, by
Lemma
B.6
a,c
we have
\Phi_{1}^{(t)}=\Theta(\frac{1}{C_{2}^{2}})
and
H_{1,2}^{(t)}=\Omega(C_{2})
, this means we can lower bound the update as

\displaystyle B_{1,1}^{(t+1)}\geq B_{1,1}^{(t)}+\Omega(\frac{\eta C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}}{C_{2}})(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{5}

since
\frac{C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}}{C_{2}}
is a constant, we know there exist some
t^{\prime}\geq 0
such that
B_{1,1}^{(t^{\prime})}\geq\Omega(\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}})
. Also recall that
T_{1,1}:=\min\{t:B_{1,1}^{(t)}\geq\Omega(\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}})\}
. So by
Lemma
G.1
, where
\eta=\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)},C_{t}=\Omega(\frac{C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}}{C_{2}})

\delta=\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)}
and
A=\Omega(\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}),\log(A/B_{1,1}^{(0)})=\widetilde{O}(1)
, we have

\displaystyle T_{1,1}=O(\frac{C_{2}}{\eta C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}})\sum_{x_{t}\leq O(\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}})}\eta C_{t}\leq O(\frac{C_{2}}{\eta C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}})\left(O(1)+\frac{\widetilde{O}(\eta)}{B_{1,1}^{(0)}}\right)\frac{1}{(B_{1,1}^{(0)})^{4}}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\eta\alpha_{1}^{6}(B_{1,1}^{(0)})^{4}})

Since
(B_{1,1}^{(0)})^{4}\geq\widetilde{\Omega}(\frac{1}{d^{2}})
from our initialization, we have
T_{1,1}\leq O(\frac{d^{2}}{\eta})
and thus
T_{1,1}
exists. Now we consider when
B_{1,1}^{(t)}\geq\Omega(\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}})
. Now by
Lemma
B.6
b,c
, we have
\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\geq\Omega((C_{2}+\alpha_{1}^{6})^{-2})
, which gives an update:

\displaystyle B_{1,1}^{(t+1)}\geq B_{1,1}^{(t)}+\Omega(\frac{\eta C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}}{(C_{2}+\alpha_{1}^{6})^{2}})(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{5}

so again by
Lemma
G.1
, choosing
C_{t}=\Omega(\frac{C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}}{(C_{2}+\alpha_{1}^{6})^{2}})
,

\displaystyle T_{1}=\frac{O((C_{2}+\alpha_{1}^{6})^{2})}{\eta C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}}\sum_{x_{t}\in[\Omega(\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}}),0.01]}\eta C_{t}\leq\left(O(1)+\frac{\widetilde{O}(\eta)}{B_{1,1}^{(T_{1,1})}}\right)\frac{\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{12})}{(B_{1,1}^{(T_{1,1})})^{4}}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{\eta(B_{1,1}^{(T_{1,1})})^{4}})\leq O(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{\eta})

where
O(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{\eta})\ll O(\frac{d^{2}}{\eta})
, so we have proved that
T_{1}
exist.
Now we begin to prove that
Induction
B.3
holds for all
t\leq T_{1}
.

Proof of
Induction
B.3
:
We first prove (b)–(d), and then come back to prove (a) and (d). At
t=0
, we know all induction holds from
Properties
B.1
. Now we suppose
Induction
B.3
holds for all iterations
\leq t-1
and prove it holds at
t
.

The growth of
B_{2,1}^{(t)}
:
Applying
Lemma
B.9
, we have for
t\leq T_{1,1}

\displaystyle B_{1,1}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\geq B_{1,1}^{(t)}+\eta(1-\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d}))\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle B_{2,1}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq B_{2,1}^{(t)}+\eta(1+O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}))\Lambda_{2,1}^{(t)}+\eta\frac{(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}}E_{1,2}^{(t)}\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}

For some
t^{\prime}_{1}:=\min\{t:B_{1,1}^{(t)}\geq\frac{\Omega(1)}{d^{0.49}}\}
, we have
E_{1,2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(B_{1,1}^{(t)}\varrho)\lesssim\frac{1}{d^{0.49}}
during
t\leq t^{\prime}_{1}
, and

\frac{(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}}E_{1,2}^{(t)}\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}\lesssim\frac{(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}}{d^{0.49}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}}\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{0.49}})\Lambda_{2,1}^{(t)}

which allow us to give an upper bound to
B_{2,1}^{(t+1)}
as

\displaystyle B_{2,1}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq(1+O(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}}))\Lambda_{2,1}^{(t)}+\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{0.49}})\Lambda_{2,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{0.49}}))\Phi_{2}^{(t)}C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(t)}(1+\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{5}

(when
t\leq t^{\prime}_{1}
)

Since we also have

\displaystyle B_{1,1}^{(t+1)}\geq(1-\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d}))\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}\geq(1-\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d}))\Phi_{1}^{(t)}C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(t)}(1-\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{5}

Since
B_{1,1}^{(0)}\geq B_{2,1}^{(0)}(1+\Omega(\frac{1}{\log d}))
, we can now apply
Corollary
G.2
to the two sequence
B_{1,1}^{(t+1)}
and
B_{2,1}^{(t+1)}
, where
S_{t}=\frac{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\mathcal{E}_{1}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{2}^{(t)}\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(t)}}(1+\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})
to get

\displaystyle B_{1,1}^{(t^{\prime}_{1})}\geq\frac{1}{d^{0.499}}\quad\text{while}\quad B_{2,1}^{(t^{\prime}_{1})}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})

Note that here the update of
B_{2,1}^{(t)}
at every step satisfies
\mathrm{sign}(B_{2,1}^{(t+1)}-B_{2,1}^{(t)})=\mathrm{sign}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})
which implies
B_{2,1}^{(t^{\prime}_{1})}=\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
. Now for every
T\in[t^{\prime}_{1},T_{1}]
, we can apply
Lemma
G.3
to get that

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in[t^{\prime}_{1},T]}\eta\frac{(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}}E_{1,2}^{(t)}\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})O(\frac{1}{B_{1,1}^{(t^{\prime}_{1})}})\max_{t\leq T}\{(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}\}\leq O(\frac{1}{d^{0.5+\Omega(1)}})

Suppose we have proved that
B_{2,1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
for each
t\leq T
, we define a new sequence

\displaystyle\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(t)}+\eta(1+\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{0.49}}))\Phi_{2}^{(t)}C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{2}^{(t)}(1+\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})(\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(t)})^{5},

\displaystyle\text{where }\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(t^{\prime}_{1})}

\displaystyle=B_{2,1}^{(t^{\prime}_{1})}+\sum_{t\in[t^{\prime}_{1},T]}\eta\frac{(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}}E_{1,2}^{(t)}\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}=(1\pm o(1))\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(t^{\prime}_{1})}

It can be directly seen that
|\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(t)}-\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(0)}|\geq|B_{2,1}^{(t)}-B_{2,1}^{(0)}|
for all
t\in[t^{\prime}_{1},T]
. Notice that now
\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(t^{\prime}_{1})}\leq d^{\Omega(1)}B_{1,1}^{(t^{\prime}_{1})}
, we can now apply
Corollary
G.2
again to get

\displaystyle|B_{2,1}^{(T)}-B_{2,1}^{(0)}|\leq|\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(T)}-\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(0)}|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}\mathsf{polylog}(d)}

(for every
T\leq T_{1,1}
)

Now we deal with
t\in[T_{1,1},T_{1}]
. During this stage, we can directly apply
Corollary
G.2
to
\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(t)}
and
B_{1,1}^{(t)}
, where
S_{t}=\frac{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}H_{1,2}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{2}^{(t)}H_{2,2}^{(t)}}\leq O(\alpha_{1}^{O(1)})
, to get that

\displaystyle|B_{2,1}^{(T)}-B_{2,1}^{(0)}|\leq|\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(T)}-\widetilde{B}_{2,1}^{(0)}|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}\mathsf{polylog}(d)}

(for every
T\leq T_{1}
)

And thus by
Lemma
B.1
, we have
B_{2,1}^{(T)}=B_{2,1}^{(0)}(1\pm o(1))
.

The growth of
B_{1,2}^{(t)}
and
B_{2,2}^{(t)}
:
By
Lemma
B.10
, we can write down the update as

\displaystyle B_{j,2}^{(t+1)}=B_{j,2}^{(t)}+\eta\left(1\pm\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{6})(E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}+(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3})\right)\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}

Since
B_{2,1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
and
E_{1,2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})B_{1,1}^{(t)},E_{2,1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d})
because we chose
\eta_{E}\leq\eta
, we only need to care about
(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}
in the update expression. Now define
t^{\prime}_{2}:=\min\{t:B_{1,1}^{(t)}\geq\Omega(\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}^{2}})\}
, we have

•

For
t\leq t^{\prime}_{2}
, by
Corollary
G.2
and setting
x_{t}=B_{1,1}^{(t)}
,
C_{t}=(1-\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d}))\Phi_{1}^{(t)}C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}H_{1,2}^{(t)}
,
S_{t}=O(\frac{\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}H_{j,1}^{(t)}}{\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}H_{1,2}^{(t)}})\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{2}^{6}}{\alpha_{1}^{6}})\ll\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)}
(by
Lemma
B.6
a,c
), we have
|B_{j,2}^{(t)}-B_{j,2}^{(0)}|\leq O(\frac{\alpha_{2}^{6}}{\alpha_{1}^{6}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}\mathsf{polylog}(d)}
for all
t\leq t^{\prime}_{2}
, which implies
B_{j,2}^{(t^{\prime}_{2})}=B_{j,2}^{(0)}\pm\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}\mathsf{polylog}(d)}\in[\Omega(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}\log d}),O(\frac{\sqrt{\log d}}{\sqrt{d}})]
by
Lemma
B.1
.

•

For
t\in[t^{\prime}_{2},T_{1}]
, we can use
Corollary
G.2
again and let
x_{t}=B_{1,1}^{(t)}
, we know
B_{1,1}^{(t^{\prime}_{2})}\geq d^{\Omega(1)}B_{2,1}^{(t^{\prime}_{2})}
. Setting
C_{t}=(1-\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d}))\Phi_{1}^{(t)}C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}H_{1,2}^{(t)}
,
S_{t}=O((1+\alpha_{1}^{6})\frac{\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}H_{j,1}^{(t)}}{\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}H_{1,2}^{(t)}})\leq O(\alpha^{O(1)})
, we can have
|B_{j,2}^{(t)}-B_{j,2}^{(t^{\prime}_{2})}|\lesssim\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}\mathsf{polylog}(d)}
, which implies
B_{j,2}^{(t)}\in[\Omega(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}\log d}),O(\frac{\sqrt{\log d}}{\sqrt{d}})]
for all
t\in[t^{\prime}_{2},T_{1}]
.

This proves
Induction
B.3
b
. Indeed, simple calculations also proves
Induction
B.3
c
, since the update of
B_{1,1}^{(t)}
is always larger than others’ during
t\leq T_{1}
.

For
Induction
B.3
d
:
From
Lemma
B.11
, we can write down the update

\displaystyle-\nabla_{E_{1,2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})

\displaystyle=O(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})\left(-C_{1}E_{1,2}^{(t)}+\widetilde{O}(\frac{(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}})+C_{2}(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)\right)

for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}=\Theta(1)
. Applying
Lemma
G.3
to
O(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})\frac{(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}}
, we can obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{t\leq T}O(\eta_{E}\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})\frac{(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}}=\frac{\eta_{E}}{\eta}\sum_{t\leq T}O(\eta\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)})\frac{(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\eta_{E}/\eta}{d^{3/2}})\frac{1}{B_{1,1}^{(0)}}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\eta_{E}/\eta}{d})

So here it suffices to notice that whenever
|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|<2\frac{C_{2}}{C_{1}}(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)
(which is obviously satisified at
t=0
), we would have

\displaystyle O(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})\left(-O(E_{1,2}^{(t)})+C_{2}(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)\right)=-O(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})\widetilde{O}(R_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)\leq O(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})

In that case, we will always have (since
E_{1,2}^{(0)}=0
)

\displaystyle E_{1,2}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{t\leq T}\widetilde{O}(\eta_{E}\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})\frac{(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}}\right|+\sum_{s\leq t}O(\eta_{E}\Lambda_{1,1}^{(s)}B_{1,1}^{(s)})(R_{1,2}^{(s)}+\varrho)\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})\frac{\eta_{E}}{\eta}B_{1,1}^{(t+1)}

Similarly for
\nabla_{E_{2,1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})
, we can write down

\displaystyle-\nabla_{E_{2,1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})=\widetilde{O}(\frac{(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}})\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}+\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{2}\Lambda_{2,\ell}^{(t)}B_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\left(-O(E_{2,1}^{(t)}R_{2}^{(t)})+O(R_{1,2}^{(t)})\right)

by
Lemma
G.3
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t\leq T_{1}}\eta_{E}\widetilde{O}(\frac{(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}})\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\eta_{E}/\eta}{d})

and since from previous comparison results we know that

\displaystyle\sum_{t\leq T_{1}}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\eta_{E}C_{2}\Lambda_{2,\ell}^{(t)}B_{2,\ell}^{(t)}=\frac{\eta_{E}}{\eta}\sum_{t\leq T_{1}}\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\eta C_{2}\Lambda_{2,\ell}^{(t)}B_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\eta_{E}/\eta}{d})

we can then prove the claim.

For
Induction
B.3
a
:
We can write down the update of
\|w_{j}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{2}
as follows:

\displaystyle\|w_{j}^{(t+1)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|w_{j}^{(t)}-\eta\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|w_{j}^{(t)}\|_{2}^{2}-\eta\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),w_{j}^{(t)}\rangle+\eta^{2}\|\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})\|_{2}^{2}

from (
A.2
) and
Induction
B.3
a,b,c
at iteration
t
and our assumption on
\xi_{p}
, we know

\|\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})\|_{2}^{2}\leq\widetilde{O}(d)

which allow us to choose
\eta\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}
to be small enough so that
\eta dT_{1}\leq\frac{1}{\eta\mathsf{poly}(d)}
. Then by
Lemma
B.8
b
, we have

\displaystyle\|w_{j}^{(t+1)}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|w_{j}^{(0)}\|_{2}^{2}\pm\eta\sum_{s\leq t}|\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(s)},E^{(s)}),w_{j}^{(s)}\rangle|\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}

\displaystyle\leq\|w_{j}^{(0)}\|_{2}^{2}\pm\eta\sum_{s\leq t}\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})|\Lambda_{1,1}^{(s)}|\sum_{j\in[2]}|E_{j,3-j}^{(s)}|\pm\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}

Since from the above analysis of the update of
B_{1,1}^{(t)}
, we know
\sum_{t\leq T_{1}}\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}\leq O(1)
. Moreover, we also know that
|B_{1,1}^{(t)}|
is increasing and
\mathrm{sign}(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)})=\mathrm{sign}(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(s)})
for any
s,t\leq T_{1}
. Thus they imply
\sum_{s\leq t}|\Lambda_{1,1}^{(s)}|=|\sum_{s\leq t}\Lambda_{1,1}^{(s)}|=O(1)
, which can be combine with
Induction
B.3
d
to prove the claim.

Proof of
Induction
B.3
e
:

We can write down the update of
R_{1,2}^{(t)}=\langle\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t)},w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle
as follows

\displaystyle\langle\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t+1)},w_{2}^{(t+1)}\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t)}-\Pi_{V^{\perp}}\eta\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{2}^{(t)}-\Pi_{V^{\perp}}\eta\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})\rangle

\displaystyle=R_{1,2}^{(t)}-\eta\langle\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle-\eta\langle\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle

\displaystyle\quad+\eta^{2}\langle\Pi_{V^{\perp}}\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})\rangle

By Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the same analysis above we have

\displaystyle|\langle\Pi_{V^{\perp}}\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})\|_{2}\|\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(d)

so by our choice of
\eta

\displaystyle\sum_{t\leq T_{1}}\eta^{2}|\langle\Pi_{V^{\perp}}\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})\rangle|\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}

and by
Lemma
B.12
we have

\displaystyle\left|-\eta\langle\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle-\eta\langle\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle\right|\leq\eta\widetilde{O}(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}B_{1,1}^{(t)})(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})

which implies

\displaystyle|\langle\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t+1)},w_{2}^{(t+1)}\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq|\langle\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(0)},w_{2}^{(0)}\rangle|+\sum_{s\leq t}\sum_{j\in[2]}\eta|\langle\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(s)},E^{(s)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{3-j}^{(s)}\rangle|+\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})+\sum_{s\leq t}\eta\widetilde{O}(\Lambda_{1,1}^{(s)}B_{1,1}^{(s)})+\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})+\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})B_{1,1}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})

which completes the proof of
Induction
B.3
. As for (a) – (e) of
Lemma
B.13
, they are just direct corrolary of our induction at
t=T_{1}
.
∎

Appendix C
Phase II: The Substitution Effect of Prediction Head

In this phase, As
B_{1,1}^{(t)}
is learned to become very large (
B_{1,1}^{(t)}\gtrsim\|w_{1}^{(t)}\|_{2}
). The focus now shift to grow
E_{2,1}^{(t)}
, because we want
C_{1}\alpha_{1}^{6}((B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{3}+E_{2,1}^{(t)}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3})^{2}
in
H_{2,1}^{(t)}
to dominate
\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{(t)}
. We can write down the gradient of
E_{2,1}^{(t)}
as

\displaystyle-\nabla_{E_{2,1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{0}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}(B_{2,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}((B_{1,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}H_{2,3-\ell}^{(t)}-(B_{2,3-\ell}^{(t)})^{3}K_{2,3-\ell}^{(t)})-\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\nabla_{E_{2,1}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{(t)}

Now let us define

\displaystyle T_{2}:=\min\{t:R_{2}^{(t)}<\frac{1}{\log d}|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|\}

(C.1)

We will prove that
E_{2,1}^{(T_{2})}
reaches at most
O(\sqrt{\eta_{E}/\eta})
and the following induction hypothesis holds throughout
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
. In this phase, the learning of
E_{2,1}^{(t)}
is much faster than the growth of the first feature
v_{1}
such that
T_{2}-T_{1}=o(T_{1}/\sqrt{d})
, which is due to the acceleration effects brought by
B_{1,1}^{(t)}=\Omega(1)
during this phase.

C.1
Induction in Phase II

We will be based on the following induction hypothesis during phase II.

Inductions C.1

(Phase II)
.

When
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
, we hypothesize the followings would hold

1.

B_{1,1}^{(t)}=\Theta(1)
,
B_{j,\ell}^{(t)}=B_{j,\ell}^{(T_{1})}(1\pm o(1))=\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
for
(j,\ell)\neq(1,1)
and
\mathrm{sign}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})=\mathrm{sign}(B_{j,\ell}^{(T_{1})})
;

2.

|R_{1,2}^{(t)}|=\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{1/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{1/2}
;

3.

R_{1}^{(t)}\in[\Omega(\frac{1}{d^{3/4}\alpha_{1}^{2}}),O(1)]
,
R_{2}^{(t)}\in[\Omega(\frac{1}{\log d}\sqrt{\eta_{E}/\eta}),O(1)]
;

4.

E_{1,2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}
and
E_{2,1}^{(t)}\leq O(\sqrt{\eta_{E}/\eta})
.

Under
Induction
C.1
, we have some results as direct corollary.

Claim C.2
.

At each iteration
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
, if
Induction
B.3
holds, then

1.

\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}=\Theta(C_{2}[R_{j}^{(t)}]^{3})
;

2.

\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)}=\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}\pm\widetilde{O}(E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2})+O((E_{j,3-j}^{(t)})^{2}[R_{3-j}^{(t)}]^{3})
for each
j\in[2]
;

Proof.

It is trivial to derive (a) from the expression of
\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}
and our assumption of
\xi_{p}
. For (b) it suffices to directly calculate the expression of
\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)}
along with
Induction
C.1
b
.
∎

Lemma C.3

(variables control in phase II)
.

In Phase II
(t\in[T_{1},T_{2}])
, if
Induction
C.1
holds, then

1.

\Phi_{1}^{(t)}=\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{1}{\alpha_{1}^{12}})
,
\Phi_{2}^{(t)}=\Theta((C_{2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3}+C_{1}\alpha_{1}^{6}(E_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2})^{-2})
;

2.

K_{1,\ell}^{(t)}=\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{\ell}^{6}/d^{3/2})
,
K_{2,\ell}^{(t)}=\widetilde{O}(E_{2,1}^{(t)}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}/d^{3/2}+\alpha_{\ell}^{6}/d^{3})

3.

H_{1,1}^{(t)}=\Theta(C_{1}\alpha_{1}^{6})
,
H_{1,2}^{(t)}=\widetilde{O}([R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3})
,
H_{2,2}^{(t)}=\Theta(C_{2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3})
,
H_{2,1}^{(t)}=\Theta(C_{2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3}+C_{1}\alpha_{1}^{6}(E_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2})
.

Proof.

The proof of (a) directly follows from
Induction
C.1
a,c
and
Claim
C.2
. The proof of (b) follows directly from the expression of
K_{j,\ell}
and
Induction
C.1
a,d
. The proof of (c) is also similar.
∎

C.2
Gradient Lemmas for Phase II

Lemma C.4

(learning prediction head
E_{1,2},E_{2,1}
in phase II)
.

If
Induction
C.1
holds at iteration
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
, then we have

\displaystyle(a)\quad-\nabla_{E_{1,2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})

\displaystyle=(1\pm\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}}{d^{3/2}}))\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}(-2E_{1,2}^{(t)}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm O(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2})

\displaystyle\qquad\pm\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\widetilde{O}(\frac{\eta_{E}/\eta}{\sqrt{d}})\max\{[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3},\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}}{d^{5/2}}\},

\displaystyle(b)\quad-\nabla_{E_{2,1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})

\displaystyle=(1\pm\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}}{d^{3/2}}))C_{0}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}\alpha_{1}^{6}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}H_{2,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\quad\pm O(\Sigma_{2,1}^{(t)})(|E_{2,1}^{(t)}|[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm O(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2})

Proof.

We first write down the gradient for
E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}
: (ignoring the time superscript
(t)
)

\displaystyle-\nabla_{E_{j,3-j}}L(W,E)

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{0}\Phi_{j}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}B_{j,\ell}^{3}(B_{3-j,\ell}^{3}H_{j,3-\ell}-B_{3-j,3-\ell}^{3}K_{j,3-\ell})-\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{j,\ell}\nabla_{E_{j,3-j}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}

where
\nabla_{E_{j,3-j}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}=\mathbb{E}\left[2\langle w_{j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{3}+2E_{j,3-j}\langle w_{3-j},\xi_{p}\rangle^{6}\right]
. Thus we have

\displaystyle\nabla_{E_{j,3-j}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)}=2E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}[R_{3-j}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm O(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}

and by
Claim
A.1
and
Induction
C.1
a
, if
(j,\ell)\neq(1,1)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}=O(\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)})\frac{(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{6}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}}\frac{\Phi_{j}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}\leq o(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\frac{\Phi_{j}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}

Therefore for
j=1
:

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{1,\ell}^{(t)}\nabla_{E_{1,2}}\mathcal{E}_{1,2}^{(t)}=(1\pm\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}}{d^{3/2}}))\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\nabla_{E_{1,2}}\mathcal{E}_{1,2}^{(t)}

Now by
Induction
C.1
a,c
and
Lemma
C.3
b,c
we have
(B_{1,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}H_{1,3-\ell}^{(t)}\leq\max\{\Theta(C_{2}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}),\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{d^{3/2}})\}
, which leads to the bounds

\displaystyle|(B_{1,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}H_{1,3-\ell}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})\max\{[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3},\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{d^{3}}\},\qquad|(B_{1,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,3-\ell}^{(t)})^{3}K_{1,3-\ell}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{3}})

which implies

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\ell\in[2]}C_{0}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}(B_{1,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}((B_{2,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}H_{1,3-\ell}^{(t)}-(B_{2,3-\ell}^{(t)})^{3}K_{1,3-\ell}^{(t)})\right|\lesssim\widetilde{O}(\frac{\eta_{E}/\eta}{\sqrt{d}})\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\max\{[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3},\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}}{d^{5/2}}\}

Combining above together, we have

\displaystyle-\nabla_{E_{1,2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle(1+o(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}}))\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}(-2E_{1,2}^{(t)}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm O(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}\pm\widetilde{O}(\frac{\eta_{E}/\eta}{\sqrt{d}})\max\{[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3},\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}}{d^{5/2}}\})

For
-\nabla_{E_{2,1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})
, the expression is slightly different, we first observe that by
Induction
C.1
a

\displaystyle\Delta_{2,2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})\Delta_{2,1}^{(t)}

Meanwhile, by
Induction
C.1
a
and
Lemma
C.3
b,c
, we have

\displaystyle\Xi_{2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}}{d^{3}})C_{0}C_{2}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3},\quad\text{}

Moreover, we can also calculate
\Sigma_{2,1}^{(t)}=C_{0}C_{2}\alpha_{1}^{6}E_{2,1}^{(t)}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{3})=\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{d^{3/2}})\Phi_{2}^{(t)}
,
\Sigma_{2,2}^{(t)}=\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{2}^{6}}{d^{3}})\Phi_{2}^{(t)}
, which gives

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\nabla_{E_{2,1}}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{(t)}=\Sigma_{2,1}^{(t)}(-\Theta(E_{2,1}^{(t)})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm O(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2})

Now we combine the above results and get

\displaystyle-\nabla_{E_{2,1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)})

\displaystyle=(1\pm\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}}{d^{3/2}}))C_{0}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}\alpha_{1}^{6}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}\mathcal{E}_{2,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\quad\pm O(\Sigma_{2,1}^{(t)})(|E_{2,1}^{(t)}|[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm O(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2})

∎

Lemma C.5

(reducing noise in phase II)
.

Suppose
Induction
C.1
holds at
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
, then

1.

\langle-\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle=\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Theta(-[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm\widetilde{O}(|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|+\frac{|E_{2,1}^{(t)}|^{2}}{d^{3/2}})(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2})
;

2.

\langle-\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle=\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}((-\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})+O(\varrho))[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{1/2}+\widetilde{O}(|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|+\frac{|E_{2,1}^{(t)}|^{2}}{d^{3/2}})R_{1}^{(t)}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{2})

And furthermore

\displaystyle(c)\quad\langle-\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\Theta([R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3})\Big(\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Theta((E_{1,2}^{(t)})^{2})+\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad\pm O\Big(\sum_{j,\ell}\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}\Big);

\displaystyle(d)\quad\langle-\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle

\displaystyle=\Big(\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Theta((E_{1,2}^{(t)})^{2})+\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\Big)(-\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})\pm O(\varrho))[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\quad+O\Big(\sum_{j,\ell}\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}R_{2}^{(t)}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{2}\Big)

Proof.

The proof can be obtained directly from some calculation using
Claim
A.1
as follows:

Proof of (a):
From (
A.2
), we can obtain that

\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle=-\sum_{j,\ell}\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}\langle\nabla_{w_{1}}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)},w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle

Now from
Claim
A.1
a
and
Induction
C.1
a
, we know
(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})
and the following

\displaystyle\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}=O(\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)})\frac{(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{6}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}}{(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{6}}\frac{\Phi_{j}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}}{d^{3/2}})\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\frac{\Phi_{j}^{(t)}}{\Phi_{1}^{(t)}}

for any
(j,\ell)\neq(1,1)

From
Induction
C.1
a,c
, we know
((B_{2,\ell}^{(t)})^{3}+E_{2,1}^{(t)}(B_{1,\ell}^{(t)})^{3})^{2}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})E_{2,1}^{(t)}
and
R_{2}^{(t)}=\Theta(1)
, which by
Claim
C.2
a,b
and
Lemma
C.3
a
gives
\Phi_{2}^{(t)}/\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{O(1)})
. Combine the bounds above, we can obtain
\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}=\widetilde{O}(E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}/d^{3/2})\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}
. We can then directly apply
Claim
A.1
to prove
Lemma
C.5
a
as follows

\displaystyle\quad\langle-\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle

\displaystyle=(1\pm\widetilde{O}(E_{1,2}^{(t)}))\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Big(-\Theta([R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3})\pm O(E_{1,2}^{(t)})(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\widetilde{O}(E_{2,1}^{(t)}/d^{3/2})\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Big(-\Theta((E_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm O(E_{2,1}^{(t)})(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Theta(\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)})\Big(-[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm\widetilde{O}(|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|+\frac{|E_{2,1}^{(t)}|^{2}}{d^{3/2}})(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}\Big)

(Since
|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|\leq d^{-\Omega(1)}
by
Induction
C.1
c,d
)

Proof of (b):
For
Lemma
C.5
b
, we can use the same analysis for
\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}
above and
Claim
A.1
(d,e)
to get (again we have used
\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}=\widetilde{O}(E_{j,3-j}^{(t)})\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}=o(\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)})
)

\displaystyle\quad\langle-\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle

\displaystyle=(1\pm\widetilde{O}(E_{1,2}^{(t)}))\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Big((-\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})\pm O(\varrho))[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{1/2}+E_{1,2}^{(t)}R_{1}^{(t)}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\widetilde{O}(E_{2,1}^{(t)}/d^{3/2})\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Big((-\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})+O(\varrho))(E_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{1/2}+E_{2,1}^{(t)}R_{1}^{(t)}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}((-\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})+O(\varrho))[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{1/2}+\widetilde{O}(|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|+\frac{|E_{2,1}^{(t)}|^{2}}{d^{3/2}})R_{1}^{(t)}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{2})

Proof of (c):
Similarly to the proof of (a), we can also expand as follows

\displaystyle\quad\langle-\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{2}^{(t)}\rangle

\displaystyle=(1\pm O(E_{1,2}^{(t)}))\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Big(-[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3}\Theta((E_{1,2}^{(t)})^{2})\pm O(E_{1,2}^{(t)})(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\Big([R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm O(E_{2,1}^{(t)})(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}\Big)

\displaystyle=-[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3}\Big(\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Theta((E_{1,2}^{(t)})^{2})+\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\Big)\pm O\Big(\sum_{j,\ell}\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}\Big)

Proof of (d):
Similarly, we can calculate (again by
\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}=\widetilde{O}(E_{j,3-j}^{(t)})\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}=o(\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)})
)

\displaystyle\quad\langle-\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),\Pi_{V^{\perp}}w_{1}^{(t)}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{1,\ell}^{(t)}\Big((-\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})\pm O(\varrho))(E_{1,2}^{(t)})^{2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{1/2}+E_{1,2}^{(t)}R_{2}^{(t)}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\Big((-\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})\pm O(\varrho))[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{1/2}+E_{2,1}^{(t)}R_{2}^{(t)}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=(1\pm\widetilde{O}(E_{1,2}^{(t)}))\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Big((-\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})\pm O(\varrho))(E_{1,2}^{(t)})^{2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{1/2}+E_{1,2}^{(t)}R_{2}^{(t)}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\Big((-\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})\pm O(\varrho))[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{1/2}+E_{1,2}^{(t)}R_{2}^{(t)}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle=\Big(\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Theta((E_{1,2}^{(t)})^{2})+\sum_{\ell\in[2]}\Sigma_{2,\ell}^{(t)}\Big)(-\Theta(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)})\pm O(\varrho))[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{5/2}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{1/2}+O\Big(\sum_{j,\ell}\Sigma_{j,\ell}^{(t)}E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}R_{2}^{(t)}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{2}\Big)

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma C.6

(learning feature
v_{2}
in phase II)
.

For each
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
, if Induction
C.1
holds at iteration
t
, then we have for each
j\in[2]
:

\displaystyle|\langle-\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),v_{2}\rangle|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{2}^{6}\alpha_{1}^{6}}{d^{5/2}})\Big(\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(|E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}|+[R_{j}^{(t)}]^{3})+\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}(|E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}|[R_{3-j}^{(t)}]^{3}+\frac{|E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}|^{2}}{d^{3/2}})\Big)

Proof.

Again as in the proof of
Lemma
B.9
, we expand the notations: (ignoring the superscript
(t)
for the RHS)

\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),v_{2}\rangle=\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}+\Gamma_{j,2}^{(t)}-\Upsilon_{j,2}^{(t)}

(C.2)

where

\displaystyle\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}H_{j,1}^{(t)}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{5}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}H_{3-j,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{j,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\left(\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}K_{j,2}^{(t)}+\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}K_{3-j,2}^{(t)}\right)

Now we further write
\Upsilon_{j,2}^{(t)}=\Upsilon_{j,2,1}^{(t)}+\Upsilon_{j,2,2}^{(t)}
, where

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{j,2,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}K_{j,2}^{(t)},\qquad\Upsilon_{j,2,2}^{(t)}=\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{3-j,2}^{(t)})^{2}K_{3-j,2}^{(t)}

According to (
C.2
), we can first compute

\displaystyle\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}-\Upsilon_{j,2,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{5}H_{j,1}^{(t)}-C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}K_{j,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{5}\left(C_{1}\alpha_{1}^{6}((B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{3})^{2}+C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}C_{1}\alpha_{2}^{6}((B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{3}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,2}^{(t)})^{3})((B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{3})

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}C_{1}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{5}\left(E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3}+(E_{j,3-j}^{(t)})^{2}(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{6}\right)

\displaystyle\quad-C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}C_{1}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}(B_{3-j,2}^{(t)})^{3}E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}\left((B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{6}+E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{3}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{5}C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j,3-j}^{(t)}

Then we can apply
Induction
C.1
a,c,d
,
Claim
C.2
a,b
and
Lemma
C.3
a,c
to get

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}-\Gamma_{j,2,1}^{(t)}|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{2}^{6}}{\alpha_{1}^{6}d^{5/2}})\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(|E_{j,3-j}^{(t)}|+[R_{j}^{(t)}]^{3})

where the last inequality is due to
Lemma
C.3
a,c
. Similarly, we can also compute for
\Gamma_{j,2}^{(t)}-\Upsilon_{j,2,2}^{(t)}
:

\displaystyle|\Gamma_{j,2}^{(t)}-\Upsilon_{j,2,2}^{(t)}|

\displaystyle\leq\left|C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}H_{3-j,1}^{(t)}\right|

\displaystyle\quad+\left|C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{2}K_{3-j,2}^{(t)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}\alpha_{2}^{6}}{d^{5/2}})\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}|E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}|([R_{3-j}^{(t)}]^{3}+\frac{|E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}|}{d^{3/2}})

This completes the proof
∎

Lemma C.7

(learning feature
v_{1}
in Phase II)
.

For each
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
, if Induction
C.1
holds at iteration
t
, then we have:

1.

\langle-\nabla_{w_{1}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),v_{1}\rangle=\Theta(\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}+\Gamma_{1,1}^{(t)}\pm\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}/d^{5/2})
;

2.

\langle-\nabla_{w_{2}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),v_{1}\rangle=\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}/d^{5/2})+\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{d})E_{1,2}^{(t)}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}

Proof.

As in the proof of
Lemma
C.6
, we expand the gradient terms:

\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),v_{1}\rangle=\Lambda_{j,2}^{(t)}+\Gamma_{j,2}^{(t)}-\Upsilon_{j,2}^{(t)}

(C.3)

where

\displaystyle\Lambda_{j,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}H_{j,2}^{(t)}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{5}

\displaystyle\Gamma_{j,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{2}H_{3-j,2}^{(t)}

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{j,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\left(\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{2}K_{j,1}^{(t)}+\Phi_{3-j}^{(t)}E_{3-j,j}^{(t)}(B_{3-j,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{2}K_{3-j,1}^{(t)}\right)

Indeed, when
j=1
, by
Induction
C.1
a
and
Lemma
C.3
a,c
, we can compute

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{5}H_{1,2}^{(t)}=\Theta(\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}

and with additionally
Lemma
C.3
b
, we also have

\displaystyle|\Upsilon_{1,1}^{(t)}|

\displaystyle=\left|C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\left(\Phi_{1}^{(t)}(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}K_{1,1}^{(t)}+\Phi_{2}^{(t)}E_{2,j}^{(t)}(B_{2,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{2}K_{2,1}^{(t)}\right)\right|\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}}{d^{5/2}})

which gives the proof of (a). For (b), we can also apply
Induction
C.1
a
and
Lemma
C.3
a,c
to get

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{2}^{(t)}H_{2,2}^{(t)}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{5}\leq\widetilde{O}(\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}/d^{5/2})

\displaystyle\Gamma_{2,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}E_{1,2}^{(t)}(B_{1,1}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}H_{1,2}^{(t)}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d})E_{1,2}^{(t)}\Phi_{1}^{(t)}\frac{[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}}{\alpha_{1}^{6}}

\displaystyle\Upsilon_{2,1}^{(t)}

\displaystyle=C_{0}\alpha_{1}^{6}\left(\Phi_{2}^{(t)}(B_{2,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}K_{2,1}^{(t)}+\Phi_{1}^{(t)}E_{1,2}^{(t)}(B_{1,2}^{(t)})^{3}(B_{2,1}^{(t)})^{2}K_{1,1}^{(t)}\right)\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{6}}{d^{4}})

this finishes the proof.
∎

C.3
At the End of Phase II

Now we shall present the main theorem of this section, which gives the result of prediction head
E_{2,1}^{(t)}
growth after the feature
v_{1}
is learned in the first stage.

Lemma C.8

(Phase II)
.

Suppose
\eta=\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}
is sufficiently small, then
Induction
C.1
holds for all iteration
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
, and at iteration
t=T_{2}
, the followings holds:

1.

B_{1,1}^{(T_{2})}=\Theta(1)
,
B_{j,\ell}^{(T_{2})}=B_{j,\ell}^{(T_{1})}(1\pm o(1))=\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
for
(j,\ell)\neq(1,1)

2.

R_{1}^{(T_{2})}\leq\widetilde{O}(\frac{1}{d^{3/4}})
,
R_{2}^{(T_{2})}=\Theta(\sqrt{\eta_{E}/\eta})
, and
\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(T_{2})}\leq\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})
;

3.

|E_{1,2}^{(T_{2})}|=\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}
and
|E_{2,1}^{(T_{2})}|=\Theta(\sqrt{\eta_{E}/\eta})

Where the part of learning
E_{2,1}^{(t)}
is what we called substitution effect. One can easily verify that
|E_{2,1}^{(t)}f_{1}(X^{(1)})|\gg|f_{2}(X^{(1)})|
when
X
is equipped with feature
v_{1}
, as stated in
Lemma
5.2
.

Proof.

We first will prove
Induction
C.1
holds for all iteration
t\in[T_{1},T_{2}]
. We shall first prove that if
Induction
C.1
continues to hold when
R_{2}^{(t)}\geq|E_{2,1}^{(t)}|
, we shall have
[R_{1}^{(t)}]
decreasing at an exponential rate.

Proof of the decrease of
R_{1}^{(t)}
:
Firstly, we write down the update of
R_{1}^{(t)}
using
Lemma
C.5
a
:

\displaystyle R_{1}^{(t+1)}=R_{1}^{(t)}+\eta\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Theta(-[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm O(|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|+\frac{|E_{2,1}^{(t)}|^{2}}{d^{3/2}})(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2})

from the expression of
\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}
in (
A.2
), and by
Induction
C.1
a
and
Lemma
C.3
a,c
, we can compute

\displaystyle\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}=\Theta(C_{0}C_{2}\Phi_{1}^{(t)})=\Theta(\frac{C_{0}C_{2}}{\alpha_{1}^{12}})

Moreover, from
Induction
C.1
c
we know that

\displaystyle(|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|+\frac{|E_{2,1}^{(t)}|^{2}}{d^{3/2}})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}

\displaystyle\leq(\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})+\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2}

\displaystyle\leq(\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})+\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}

Therefore whenever
R_{1}^{(t)}\geq\frac{\alpha_{1}^{18}}{d^{3/4}}
(which
t\leq T_{2}
suffices), we shall have always have

\displaystyle(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)(\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{1}{d^{3/2}})+\widetilde{O}(\varrho+\frac{1}{\sqrt{d}})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}\leq o([R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3})

which implies, if we set
T^{\prime}_{2}:=\min\{t:R_{1}^{(t)}\geq\frac{1}{d^{3/4}\alpha_{1}^{2}}\}
, then for all
t\in[T_{1},T^{\prime}_{2}]
, we will have

\displaystyle R_{1}^{(t+1)}

\displaystyle=R_{1}^{(t)}+\eta\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)}\Theta(-[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}\pm O(|E_{1,2}^{(t)}|+\frac{|E_{2,1}^{(t)}|^{2}}{d^{3/2}})(\overline{R}_{1,2}^{(t)}+\varrho)[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3/2}[R_{2}^{(t)}]^{3/2})

\displaystyle=R_{1}^{(t)}-\Theta(\eta\Sigma_{1,1}^{(t)})[R_{1}^{(t)}]^{3}

(C.4)

\displaystyle\leq R_{1}^{(t)}(1-\Theta(\frac{\eta C_{0}C_{2}}{\alpha_{1}^{12}})\frac{1}{d^{3/2}\alpha_{1}^{2}})

From the last inequality we know that after
T_{2}=T_{1}+\widetilde{\Theta}(\frac{d^{1.5}}{\eta\alpha_{1}^{\Omega(1)}})
, we shall have
R_{1}^{(t)}\leq O(\frac{\alpha_{1}^{O(1)}}{d^{3/4}})
. Moreover, suppose
T^{\prime}_{2}<T_{2}
, (which just mean
R_{1}^{(s)}\leq O(\frac{1}{d^{3/4}\alpha_{1}^{2}})
for some iteration
