). When trained with
E^{(t)}\equiv I_{m}
, we have for some sufficiently large
t\geq\mathsf{poly}(d)/\eta
:

\displaystyle L(W^{(t)},I_{m})\leq\mathsf{OPT}+\frac{1}{\mathsf{poly}(d)}

Proof of
Theorem
F.1
.

The proof is easy to obtain since it is very similar to some proofs in previous sections, and we only sketch it here. Indeed, using the calculations in
Lemma
D.5
and
Lemma
D.4
and set
E_{i,j}^{(t)},i\neq j\in[m]
to zero. We shall have (note that here
\mathcal{E}_{j,r}^{(t)}\equiv\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}
for any
r\neq j
)

\displaystyle\langle-\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(t)},E^{(t)}),v_{\ell}\rangle=C_{0}C_{2}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{5}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(t)}=\Theta(C_{0}C_{2}\alpha_{\ell}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,\ell}^{(t)})^{5}[R_{j}^{(t)}]^{3})

Now we can go through the similar induction arguments as in the proof of
Lemma
B.13
(with TPM lemma to distinguish the learning speed) to obtain that for each
j\in[m]
:

\displaystyle|B_{j,1}^{(t)}|=\Theta(1),\quad|B_{j,2}^{(t)}|=|B_{j,2}^{(0)}|(1\pm o(1)),\quad\forall j\in[m]

(when
t\geq\frac{d^{2}}{\eta}
)

When this is proven, we can also reuse the calculations as in the proof of
Lemma
C.5
to obtain that

\displaystyle R_{j}^{(t+1)}=R_{j}^{(t)}(1-\Theta(\eta\Sigma_{j,1}^{(t)})[R_{j}^{(t)}]^{2})=R_{j}^{(t)}(1-\Theta(\eta C_{0}C_{2}\alpha_{1}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(t)}(B_{j,1}^{(t)})^{6}[R_{j}^{(t)}]^{2}),\quad\forall j\in[m]

So again after some
t=\widetilde{O}(\frac{d^{2}}{\eta})
, we shall have
R_{j}^{(t)}\leq O(\frac{d^{o(1)}}{d})
. While the decrease of
R_{j}^{(t)}
is happening, we can make induction that
|B_{j,2}^{(t)}|=|B_{j,2}^{(0)}|(1\pm o(1))
, since if it holds for all previous iterations before
t
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{s\leq t-1}\eta|\langle-\nabla_{w_{j}}L(W^{(s)},E^{(s)}),v_{2}\rangle|

\displaystyle=\sum_{s\leq t-1}\eta C_{0}\alpha_{2}^{6}\Phi_{j}^{(s)}|B_{j,2}^{(s)}|^{5}C_{2}\mathcal{E}_{j}^{(s)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\text{①}}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)}|B_{j,2}^{(0)}|

where ① is due to
Corollary
G.2
, where
x_{t}=|B_{j,1}^{(t)}|
and
y_{t}=|B_{j,2}^{(t)}|
and
S_{t}\leq\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)}
,
y_{0}\leq O(\log d)x_{0}
. which finishes the proof.
∎

Appendix G
Tensor Power Method Bounds

In this section, we give two lemmas related to the tensor power method that can help us in previous sections’ proofs.

Lemma G.1

(TPM, adapted from
[
3
]
)
.

Consider an increasing sequence
x_{t}\geq 0
defined by
x_{t+1}=x_{t}+\eta C_{t}x_{t}^{q}
for some integer
q\geq 3
and
C_{t}>0
, and suippose for some
A>0
there exist
t^{\prime}\geq 0
such that
x_{t^{\prime}}\geq A
. Then for every
\delta>0
, and every
\eta\in(0,1)
:

\displaystyle\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}

\displaystyle\geq\left(\frac{\delta(1+\delta)^{-1}}{(1+\delta)^{q-1}-1}\left(1-\left(\frac{(1+\delta)x_{0}}{A}\right)^{q-1}\right)-\frac{O(\eta A^{q})}{x_{0}}\frac{\log(A/x_{0})}{\log(1+\delta)}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-1}}

\displaystyle\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{(1+\delta)^{q-1}}{q-1}+\frac{O(\eta A^{q})}{x_{0}}\frac{\log(A/x_{0})}{\log(1+\delta)}\right)\cdot\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-1}}

This lemma has a corollary:

Corollary G.2

(TPM, from
[
3
]
)
.

Let
q\geq 3
be a constant and
x_{0},y_{0}=o(1)
and
A=O(1)
. Let
\{x_{t},y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be two positive sequences updated as

•

x_{t+1}=x_{t}+\eta C_{t}x_{t}^{q}
for some
C_{t}>0
;

•

y_{t+1}=y_{t}+\eta S_{t}C_{t}y_{t}^{q}
for some
S_{t}>0
.

Suppose
x_{0}\geq y_{0}(\max_{t:x_{t}\leq A}S_{t})^{\frac{1}{q-1}}(1+\frac{1}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)})
, then
y_{t}\leq\widetilde{O}(y_{0})
for all
t
such that
x_{t}\leq A
. Moreover, if
x_{0}\geq y_{0}(\max_{t:x_{t}\leq A}S_{t})^{\frac{1}{q-1}}\log(d)
, we would have
|y_{t}-y_{0}|\lesssim\frac{|y_{0}|}{\mathsf{polylog}(d)}
.

Moreover, we prove the following lemma for comparing the updates of different variables.

Lemma G.3

(TPM of different degrees)
.

Consider an increasing sequences
x_{t}\geq 0
defined by

x_{t+1}=x_{t}+\eta C_{t}x_{t}^{q}

for some integer
q>q^{\prime}\geq 3
and
q^{\prime}\leq q-2
, and
C_{t}>0
, and further suppose given
A=O(1)
, there exists
t^{\prime}\geq 0,x_{t^{\prime}}\geq A
. Then for every
\delta>0
and every
\eta\in(0,1)
:

\displaystyle\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}x_{t}^{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\delta)^{q^{\prime}}\left(O(1)+\eta bA^{q}\right)\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}

\displaystyle\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}x_{t}^{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\geq(1+\delta)^{-q^{\prime}}\left(\delta(1+\delta)^{-1}\frac{1-(1+\delta)^{-b(q-q^{\prime}-1)}}{1-(1+\delta)^{-(q-q^{\prime}-1)}}-\eta bA^{q}\right)\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}

where
b=\Theta(\log(A/x_{0})/\log(1+\delta))
. When
A=x_{0}d^{\Theta(1)}
,
\eta=o(\frac{1}{A^{q}\delta})
and
q=O(1)
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}x_{t}^{q^{\prime}}=\Theta(\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}})

Proof.

For every
g\in 0,1,\dots
, we define
\mathcal{T}_{g}:=\min\{t:x_{t}\geq(1+\delta)^{g}x_{0}\}
. and define
b:=\min\{g:(1+\delta)^{g}\geq A\}
, we can write down the following two inequalities according to the update of
x_{t}
:

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in[\mathcal{T}_{g},\mathcal{T}_{g+1}]}\eta C_{t}[(1+\delta)^{g}x_{0}]^{q}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\delta)x_{\mathcal{T}_{g}}-x_{\mathcal{T}_{g}}+\eta A^{q}\leq\delta(1+\delta)^{g}x_{0}+\eta A^{q}

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in[\mathcal{T}_{g},\mathcal{T}_{g+1}]}\eta C_{t}[(1+\delta)^{g+1}x_{0}]^{q}

\displaystyle\geq(1+\delta)x_{\mathcal{T}_{g}}-x_{\mathcal{T}_{g}}-\eta A^{q}\geq\delta(1+\delta)^{g}x_{0}-\eta A^{q}

where
g+1\leq b
. Dividing both sides by
[(1+\delta)^{g}x_{0}]^{q-q^{\prime}}
in the first inequality and
[(1+\delta)^{g+1}x_{0}]^{q-q^{\prime}}
in the second, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in[\mathcal{T}_{g},\mathcal{T}_{g+1}]}\eta C_{t}[(1+\delta)^{g}x_{0}]^{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\delta}{(1+\delta)^{g(q-q^{\prime}-1)}}\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}+\frac{\eta A^{q}}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in[\mathcal{T}_{g},\mathcal{T}_{g+1}]}\eta C_{t}[(1+\delta)^{g+1}x_{0}]^{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\delta(1+\delta)^{-1}}{(1+\delta)^{(g+1)(q-q^{\prime}-1)}}\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}-\frac{\eta A^{q}}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}

Therefore if we sum over
g=0,\dots,b
, then

\displaystyle\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}x_{t}^{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}[(1+\delta)^{g+1}x_{0}]^{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(1+\delta)^{q^{\prime}}\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}[(1+\delta)^{g}x_{0}]^{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\delta)^{q^{\prime}}\sum_{0\leq g\leq b}\left(\frac{\delta}{(1+\delta)^{g(q-q^{\prime}-1)}}\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}+\frac{\eta A^{q}}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}\right)

\displaystyle=(1+\delta)^{q^{\prime}}O\left(\frac{\delta}{(1+\delta)^{q-q^{\prime}-1}-1}+\eta bA^{q}\right)\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\delta)^{q^{\prime}}O\left(\frac{1}{q-q^{\prime}-1}+\eta bA^{q}\right)\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}

For the lower bound, we also have

\displaystyle\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}x_{t}^{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\geq(1+\delta)^{-q^{\prime}}\sum_{t\geq 0,x_{t}\leq A}\eta C_{t}[(1+\delta)^{g+1}x_{0}]^{q^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\geq(1+\delta)^{-q^{\prime}}\sum_{0\leq g\leq b}\left(\frac{\delta(1+\delta)^{-1}}{(1+\delta)^{(g+1)(q-q^{\prime}-1)}}-\eta A^{q}\right)\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}

\displaystyle=(1+\delta)^{-q^{\prime}}\left(\delta(1+\delta)^{-1}\frac{1-(1+\delta)^{-b(q-q^{\prime}-1)}}{1-(1+\delta)^{-(q-q^{\prime}-1)}}-\eta bA^{q}\right)\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}

\displaystyle=(1+\delta)^{-q^{\prime}}\left(\delta(1+\delta)^{-1}\frac{1-(1+\delta)^{-b(q-q^{\prime}-1)}}{1-(1+\delta)^{-(q-q^{\prime}-1)}}-\eta bA^{q}\right)\frac{1}{x_{0}^{q-q^{\prime}-1}}

Inserting
b=\Theta(\log(A/x_{0})/\log(1+\delta))
proves the lower bound. For the last one we can choose
\delta=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\log d}}
to get:

\displaystyle b=\Theta(\mathsf{polylog}(d)),\quad\frac{\delta(1-(1+\delta)^{-b(q-q^{\prime}-1)})}{1-(1+\delta)^{-(q-q^{\prime}-1)}}=\Omega(1),\quad(1+\delta)^{-q^{\prime}}=\Omega(1),

which proves the claim.
∎

60
